data-container="article" style="grid-area:content" data-search="article-body"><div id="article-contents"><div class="MarkdownContent_markdownBody__v5MYy markdown-body"><p>GitHub Copilot can provide responses that are tailored to your personal preferences, the way your team works, the tools you use, or the specifics of your project, if you provide it with enough context to do so. Instead of repeatedly adding this contextual detail to your prompts, you can create custom instructions that automatically add this information for you. The additional information is not displayed, but is available to Copilot to allow it to generate higher quality responses.</p>
<h2 id="types-of-custom-instructions" tabindex="-1"><a class="heading-link" href="#types-of-custom-instructions">Types of custom instructions<span class="heading-link-symbol" aria-hidden="true"></span></a></h2>
<p>GitHub Copilot CLI supports instructions from the following locations.</p>
<p>Unless noted in the table below, Copilot CLI discovers repository and agent instruction files in the <strong>standard locations</strong>: the repository root, the current working directory, intermediate directories between them, and any directories nested in the path of a file it is working on. Modular instruction files (those matching <code>*.instructions.md</code>) are path-specific—a file with an <code>applyTo</code> value applies only to matching files.</p>









































<table aria-labelledby="types-of-custom-instructions"><thead><tr><th scope="col">Location</th><th scope="col">Scope and behavior</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td><code>$HOME/<wbr/>.copilot/<wbr/>copilot-instructions.md</code></td><td>User-level instructions that apply across repositories.</td></tr><tr><td><code>$HOME/<wbr/>.copilot/<wbr/>instructions/<wbr/>**/<wbr/>*.instructions.md</code></td><td>Modular user-level instructions.</td></tr><tr><td><code>.github/<wbr/>copilot-instructions.md</code></td><td>Repository-wide instructions, discovered in the standard locations.</td></tr><tr><td><code>.github/<wbr/>instructions/<wbr/>**/<wbr/>*.instructions.md</code></td><td>Modular repository instructions, discovered in the standard locations but not intermediate directories.</td></tr><tr><td><code>AGENTS.md</code></td><td>Agent instructions, discovered in the standard locations. For more information, see the <a href="https://github.com/agentsmd/agents.md">agentsmd/agents.md repository</a>.</td></tr><tr><td><code>CLAUDE.md</code></td><td>Agent instructions, discovered in the standard locations. Copilot CLI also uses <code>.claude/CLAUDE.md</code>.</td></tr><tr><td><code>GEMINI.md</code></td><td>Agent instructions, discovered in the standard locations.</td></tr><tr><td>Directories listed in <code>COPILOT_CUSTOM_<wbr/>INSTRUCTIONS_<wbr/>DIRS</code></td><td>Additional <code>AGENTS.md</code> and <code>*.instructions.md</code> files. Separate multiple directories with commas.</td></tr></tbody></table>
<p>If you set the <code>COPILOT_HOME</code> environment variable, Copilot CLI uses that directory instead of <code>$HOME/.copilot</code> for both user-level instruction locations.</p>
<p>Use the <code>/instructions</code> command to view the instruction files discovered for the current session and enable or disable individual files.</p>
<h2 id="how-multiple-instruction-files-interact" tabindex="-1"><a class="heading-link" href="#how-multiple-instruction-files-interact">How multiple instruction files interact<span class="heading-link-symbol" aria-hidden="true"></span></a></h2>
<p>When multiple applicable user-level and repository instruction files exist, Copilot CLI combines their instructions. It removes duplicate copies of identical user-level <code>copilot-instructions.md</code>, repository-wide, and agent instructions, but does not define a general precedence order between these files. Avoid conflicting instructions.</p>
<p>Path-specific instructions are included only when their <code>applyTo</code> value matches a file that Copilot CLI is working with. An instruction file that you disable using <code>/instructions</code> is not included.</p>
<h2 id="creating-repository-wide-custom-instructions" tabindex="-1"><a class="heading-link" href="#creating-repository-wide-custom-instructions">Creating repository-wide custom instructions<span class="heading-link-symbol" aria-hidden="true"></span></a></h2>
<ol>
<li>
<p>In the root of your repository, create a file named <code>.github/copilot-instructions.md</code>.</p>
<p>Create the <code>.github</code> directory if it does not already exist.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Add natural language instructions to the file, in Markdown format.</p>
<p>Whitespace between instructions is ignored, so the instructions can be written as a single paragraph, each on a new line, or separated by blank lines for legibility.</p>
<p>For help on writing effective custom instructions, see <a href="/en/copilot/concepts/prompting/response-customization#writing-effective-custom-instructions">About customizing GitHub Copilot responses</a>.</p>
</li>
</ol>
<h3 id="referencing-other-files" tabindex="-1"><a class="heading-link" href="#referencing-other-files">Referencing other files<span class="heading-link-symbol" aria-hidden="true"></span></a></h3>
<p>In <code>.github/copilot-instructions.md</code>, <code>AGENTS.md</code>, or <code>CLAUDE.md</code>, use <code>@</code> followed by a relative path to include another file. Copilot CLI reads the referenced file immediately and supports references within referenced files.</p>
<p>Referenced files must remain within the repository, or within the custom instructions directory for local instructions. Absolute paths and paths beginning with <code>~/</code> are not loaded. File references are not expanded in <code>GEMINI.md</code> or <code>*.instructions.md</code> files.</p>
<h2 id="creating-path-specific-custom-instructions" tabindex="-1"><a class="heading-link" href="#creating-path-specific-custom-instructions">Creating path-specific custom instructions<span class="heading-link-symbol" aria-hidden="true"></span></a></h2>
<ol>
<li>
<p>Create the <code>.github/instructions</code> directory if it does not already exist.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Optionally, create subdirectories of <code>.github/instructions</code> to organize your instruction files.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Create one or more <code>NAME.instructions.md</code> files, where <code>NAME</code> indicates the purpose of the instructions. The file name must end with <code>.instructions.md</code>.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>At the start of the file, create a frontmatter block containing the <code>applyTo</code> keyword. Use glob syntax to specify what files or directories the instructions apply to.</p>
<p>For example:</p>
<pre><code class="hljs language-markdown">---
<span class="hljs-section">applyTo: &quot;app/models/<span class="hljs-strong">**/<span class="hljs-emphasis">*.rb&quot;
---
</span></span></span></code></pre>
<p>You can specify multiple patterns by separating them with commas. For example, to apply the instructions to all TypeScript files in the repository, you could use the following frontmatter block:</p>
<pre><code class="hljs language-markdown">---
<span class="hljs-section">applyTo: &quot;<span class="hljs-strong">**/<span class="hljs-emphasis">*.ts,*</span><span class="hljs-emphasis">*/*</span>.tsx&quot;
---
</span></span></code></pre>
<p>Glob examples:</p>
<ul>
<li><code>*</code> - will all match all files in the current directory.</li>
<li><code>**</code> or <code>**/*</code> - will all match all files in all directories.</li>
<li><code>*.py</code> - will match all <code>.py</code> files in the current directory.</li>
<li><code>**/*.py</code> - will recursively match all <code>.py</code> files in all directories.</li>
<li><code>src/*.py</code> - will match all <code>.py</code> files in the <code>src</code> directory. For example, <code>src/foo.py</code> and <code>src/bar.py</code> but <em>not</em> <code>src/foo/bar.py</code>.</li>
<li><code>src/**/*.py</code> - will recursively match all <code>.py</code> files in the <code>src</code> directory. For example, <code>src/foo.py</code>, <code>src/foo/bar.py</code>, and <code>src/foo/bar/baz.py</code>.</li>
<li><code>**/subdir/**/*.py</code> - will recursively match all <code>.py</code> files in any <code>subdir</code> directory at any depth. For example, <code>subdir/foo.py</code>, <code>subdir/nested/bar.py</code>, <code>parent/subdir/baz.py</code>, and <code>deep/parent/subdir/nested/qux.py</code>, but <em>not</em> <code>foo.py</code> at a path that does not contain a <code>subdir</code> directory.</li>
</ul>
</li>
<li>
<p>Optionally, to prevent the file from being used by either Copilot cloud agent or Copilot code review, add the <code>excludeAgent</code> keyword to the frontmatter block. Use either <code>&quot;code-review&quot;</code> or <code>&quot;cloud-agent&quot;</code>.</p>
<p>For example, the following file will only be read by Copilot cloud agent.</p>
<pre><code class="hljs language-markdown">---
applyTo: &quot;<span class="hljs-strong">**&quot;
excludeAgent: &quot;code-review&quot;
---
</span></code></pre>
<p>If the <code>excludeAgent</code> keyword is not included in the front matterblock, both Copilot code review and Copilot cloud agent will use your instructions.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Add your custom instructions in natural language, using Markdown format. Whitespace between instructions is ignored, so the instructions can be written as a single paragraph, each on a new line, or separated by blank lines for legibility.</p>
</li>
</ol>
<h2 id="custom-instructions-in-use" tabindex="-1"><a class="heading-link" href="#custom-instructions-in-use">Custom instructions in use<span class="heading-link-symbol" aria-hidden="true"></span></a></h2>
<p>Changes you make to custom instructions files are not immediately available for use in active CLI sessions. To apply your changes, exit the current session and then either resume it (for example, run <code>copilot --continue</code>), or start a new session (for example, use <code>/new</code> from within an interactive session).</p>

custom instructions for GitHub Copilot CLI","intro":"\u003cp\u003eGive Copilot additional context on how to understand your project and how to build, test and validate its changes.\u003c/p\u003e","effectiveDate":"","renderedPage":"\u003cp\u003eGitHub Copilot can provide responses that are tailored to your personal preferences, the way your team works, the tools you use, or the specifics of your project, if you provide it with enough context to do so. Instead of repeatedly adding this contextual detail to your prompts, you can create custom instructions that automatically add this information for you. The additional information is not displayed, but is available to Copilot to allow it to generate higher quality responses.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003ch2 id=\"types-of-custom-instructions\" tabindex=\"-1\"\u003e\u003ca class=\"heading-link\" href=\"#types-of-custom-instructions\"\u003eTypes of custom instructions\u003cspan class=\"heading-link-symbol\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/h2\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eGitHub Copilot CLI supports instructions from the following locations.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eUnless noted in the table below, Copilot CLI discovers repository and agent instruction files in the \u003cstrong\u003estandard locations\u003c/strong\u003e: the repository root, the current working directory, intermediate directories between them, and any directories nested in the path of a file it is working on. Modular instruction files (those matching \u003ccode\u003e*.instructions.md\u003c/code\u003e) are path-specific—a file with an \u003ccode\u003eapplyTo\u003c/code\u003e value applies only to matching files.\u003c/p\u003e\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\u003ctable aria-labelledby=\"types-of-custom-instructions\"\u003e\u003cthead\u003e\u003ctr\u003e\u003cth scope=\"col\"\u003eLocation\u003c/th\u003e\u003cth scope=\"col\"\u003eScope and behavior\u003c/th\u003e\u003c/tr\u003e\u003c/thead\u003e\u003ctbody\u003e\u003ctr\u003e\u003ctd\u003e\u003ccode\u003e$HOME/\u003cwbr\u003e.copilot/\u003cwbr\u003ecopilot-instructions.md\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/td\u003e\u003ctd\u003eUser-level instructions that apply across repositories.\u003c/td\u003e\u003c/tr\u003e\u003ctr\u003e\u003ctd\u003e\u003ccode\u003e$HOME/\u003cwbr\u003e.copilot/\u003cwbr\u003einstructions/\u003cwbr\u003e**/\u003cwbr\u003e*.instructions.md\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/td\u003e\u003ctd\u003eModular user-level instructions.\u003c/td\u003e\u003c/tr\u003e\u003ctr\u003e\u003ctd\u003e\u003ccode\u003e.github/\u003cwbr\u003ecopilot-instructions.md\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/td\u003e\u003ctd\u003eRepository-wide instructions, discovered in the standard locations.\u003c/td\u003e\u003c/tr\u003e\u003ctr\u003e\u003ctd\u003e\u003ccode\u003e.github/\u003cwbr\u003einstructions/\u003cwbr\u003e**/\u003cwbr\u003e*.instructions.md\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/td\u003e\u003ctd\u003eModular repository instructions, discovered in the standard locations but not intermediate directories.\u003c/td\u003e\u003c/tr\u003e\u003ctr\u003e\u003ctd\u003e\u003ccode\u003eAGENTS.md\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/td\u003e\u003ctd\u003eAgent instructions, discovered in the standard locations. For more information, see the \u003ca href=\"https://github.com/agentsmd/agents.md\"\u003eagentsmd/agents.md repository\u003c/a\u003e.\u003c/td\u003e\u003c/tr\u003e\u003ctr\u003e\u003ctd\u003e\u003ccode\u003eCLAUDE.md\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/td\u003e\u003ctd\u003eAgent instructions, discovered in the standard locations. Copilot CLI also uses \u003ccode\u003e.claude/CLAUDE.md\u003c/code\u003e.\u003c/td\u003e\u003c/tr\u003e\u003ctr\u003e\u003ctd\u003e\u003ccode\u003eGEMINI.md\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/td\u003e\u003ctd\u003eAgent instructions, discovered in the standard locations.\u003c/td\u003e\u003c/tr\u003e\u003ctr\u003e\u003ctd\u003eDirectories listed in \u003ccode\u003eCOPILOT_CUSTOM_\u003cwbr\u003eINSTRUCTIONS_\u003cwbr\u003eDIRS\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/td\u003e\u003ctd\u003eAdditional \u003ccode\u003eAGENTS.md\u003c/code\u003e and \u003ccode\u003e*.instructions.md\u003c/code\u003e files. Separate multiple directories with commas.\u003c/td\u003e\u003c/tr\u003e\u003c/tbody\u003e\u003c/table\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eIf you set the \u003ccode\u003eCOPILOT_HOME\u003c/code\u003e environment variable, Copilot CLI uses that directory instead of \u003ccode\u003e$HOME/.copilot\u003c/code\u003e for both user-level instruction locations.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eUse the \u003ccode\u003e/instructions\u003c/code\u003e command to view the instruction files discovered for the current session and enable or disable individual files.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003ch2 id=\"how-multiple-instruction-files-interact\" tabindex=\"-1\"\u003e\u003ca class=\"heading-link\" href=\"#how-multiple-instruction-files-interact\"\u003eHow multiple instruction files interact\u003cspan class=\"heading-link-symbol\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/h2\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eWhen multiple applicable user-level and repository instruction files exist, Copilot CLI combines their instructions. It removes duplicate copies of identical user-level \u003ccode\u003ecopilot-instructions.md\u003c/code\u003e, repository-wide, and agent instructions, but does not define a general precedence order between these files. Avoid conflicting instructions.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003ePath-specific instructions are included only when their \u003ccode\u003eapplyTo\u003c/code\u003e value matches a file that Copilot CLI is working with. An instruction file that you disable using \u003ccode\u003e/instructions\u003c/code\u003e is not included.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003ch2 id=\"creating-repository-wide-custom-instructions\" tabindex=\"-1\"\u003e\u003ca class=\"heading-link\" href=\"#creating-repository-wide-custom-instructions\"\u003eCreating repository-wide custom instructions\u003cspan class=\"heading-link-symbol\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/h2\u003e\n\u003col\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eIn the root of your repository, create a file named \u003ccode\u003e.github/copilot-instructions.md\u003c/code\u003e.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eCreate the \u003ccode\u003e.github\u003c/code\u003e directory if it does not already exist.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eAdd natural language instructions to the file, in Markdown format.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eWhitespace between instructions is ignored, so the instructions can be written as a single paragraph, each on a new line, or separated by blank lines for legibility.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eFor help on writing effective custom instructions, see \u003ca href=\"/en/copilot/concepts/prompting/response-customization#writing-effective-custom-instructions\"\u003eAbout customizing GitHub Copilot responses\u003c/a\u003e.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003c/ol\u003e\n\u003ch3 id=\"referencing-other-files\" tabindex=\"-1\"\u003e\u003ca class=\"heading-link\" href=\"#referencing-other-files\"\u003eReferencing other files\u003cspan class=\"heading-link-symbol\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/h3\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eIn \u003ccode\u003e.github/copilot-instructions.md\u003c/code\u003e, \u003ccode\u003eAGENTS.md\u003c/code\u003e, or \u003ccode\u003eCLAUDE.md\u003c/code\u003e, use \u003ccode\u003e@\u003c/code\u003e followed by a relative path to include another file. Copilot CLI reads the referenced file immediately and supports references within referenced files.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eReferenced files must remain within the repository, or within the custom instructions directory for local instructions. Absolute paths and paths beginning with \u003ccode\u003e~/\u003c/code\u003e are not loaded. File references are not expanded in \u003ccode\u003eGEMINI.md\u003c/code\u003e or \u003ccode\u003e*.instructions.md\u003c/code\u003e files.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003ch2 id=\"creating-path-specific-custom-instructions\" tabindex=\"-1\"\u003e\u003ca class=\"heading-link\" href=\"#creating-path-specific-custom-instructions\"\u003eCreating path-specific custom instructions\u003cspan class=\"heading-link-symbol\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/h2\u003e\n\u003col\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eCreate the \u003ccode\u003e.github/instructions\u003c/code\u003e directory if it does not already exist.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eOptionally, create subdirectories of \u003ccode\u003e.github/instructions\u003c/code\u003e to organize your instruction files.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eCreate one or more \u003ccode\u003eNAME.instructions.md\u003c/code\u003e files, where \u003ccode\u003eNAME\u003c/code\u003e indicates the purpose of the instructions. The file name must end with \u003ccode\u003e.instructions.md\u003c/code\u003e.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eAt the start of the file, create a frontmatter block containing the \u003ccode\u003eapplyTo\u003c/code\u003e keyword. Use glob syntax to specify what files or directories the instructions apply to.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eFor example:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cpre\u003e\u003ccode class=\"hljs language-markdown\"\u003e---\n\u003cspan class=\"hljs-section\"\u003eapplyTo: \"app/models/\u003cspan class=\"hljs-strong\"\u003e**/\u003cspan class=\"hljs-emphasis\"\u003e*.rb\"\n---\n\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/pre\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eYou can specify multiple patterns by separating them with commas. For example, to apply the instructions to all TypeScript files in the repository, you could use the following frontmatter block:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cpre\u003e\u003ccode class=\"hljs language-markdown\"\u003e---\n\u003cspan class=\"hljs-section\"\u003eapplyTo: \"\u003cspan class=\"hljs-strong\"\u003e**/\u003cspan class=\"hljs-emphasis\"\u003e*.ts,*\u003c/span\u003e\u003cspan class=\"hljs-emphasis\"\u003e*/*\u003c/span\u003e.tsx\"\n---\n\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/pre\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eGlob examples:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cul\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003ccode\u003e*\u003c/code\u003e - will all match all files in the current directory.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003ccode\u003e**\u003c/code\u003e or \u003ccode\u003e**/*\u003c/code\u003e - will all match all files in all directories.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003ccode\u003e*.py\u003c/code\u003e - will match all \u003ccode\u003e.py\u003c/code\u003e files in the current directory.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003ccode\u003e**/*.py\u003c/code\u003e - will recursively match all \u003ccode\u003e.py\u003c/code\u003e files in all directories.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003ccode\u003esrc/*.py\u003c/code\u003e - will match all \u003ccode\u003e.py\u003c/code\u003e files in the \u003ccode\u003esrc\u003c/code\u003e directory. For example, \u003ccode\u003esrc/foo.py\u003c/code\u003e and \u003ccode\u003esrc/bar.py\u003c/code\u003e but \u003cem\u003enot\u003c/em\u003e \u003ccode\u003esrc/foo/bar.py\u003c/code\u003e.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003ccode\u003esrc/**/*.py\u003c/code\u003e - will recursively match all \u003ccode\u003e.py\u003c/code\u003e files in the \u003ccode\u003esrc\u003c/code\u003e directory. For example, \u003ccode\u003esrc/foo.py\u003c/code\u003e, \u003ccode\u003esrc/foo/bar.py\u003c/code\u003e, and \u003ccode\u003esrc/foo/bar/baz.py\u003c/code\u003e.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003ccode\u003e**/subdir/**/*.py\u003c/code\u003e - will recursively match all \u003ccode\u003e.py\u003c/code\u003e files in any \u003ccode\u003esubdir\u003c/code\u003e directory at any depth. For example, \u003ccode\u003esubdir/foo.py\u003c/code\u003e, \u003ccode\u003esubdir/nested/bar.py\u003c/code\u003e, \u003ccode\u003eparent/subdir/baz.py\u003c/code\u003e, and \u003ccode\u003edeep/parent/subdir/nested/qux.py\u003c/code\u003e, but \u003cem\u003enot\u003c/em\u003e \u003ccode\u003efoo.py\u003c/code\u003e at a path that does not contain a \u003ccode\u003esubdir\u003c/code\u003e directory.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003c/ul\u003e\n\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eOptionally, to prevent the file from being used by either Copilot cloud agent or Copilot code review, add the \u003ccode\u003eexcludeAgent\u003c/code\u003e keyword to the frontmatter block. Use either \u003ccode\u003e\"code-review\"\u003c/code\u003e or \u003ccode\u003e\"cloud-agent\"\u003c/code\u003e.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eFor example, the following file will only be read by Copilot cloud agent.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cpre\u003e\u003ccode class=\"hljs language-markdown\"\u003e---\napplyTo: \"\u003cspan class=\"hljs-strong\"\u003e**\"\nexcludeAgent: \"code-review\"\n---\n\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/pre\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eIf the \u003ccode\u003eexcludeAgent\u003c/code\u003e keyword is not included in the front matterblock, both Copilot code review and Copilot cloud agent will use your instructions.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eAdd your custom instructions in natural language, using Markdown format. Whitespace between instructions is ignored, so the instructions can be written as a single paragraph, each on a new line, or separated by blank lines for legibility.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003c/ol\u003e\n\u003ch2 id=\"custom-instructions-in-use\" tabindex=\"-1\"\u003e\u003ca class=\"heading-link\" href=\"#custom-instructions-in-use\"\u003eCustom instructions in use\u003cspan class=\"heading-link-symbol\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/h2\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eChanges you make to custom instructions files are not immediately available for use in active CLI sessions.
